# Publication scientific repair and accepted ownership

Created 17 October 2026; B5/E1 source checkpoint 18 October 2026. **Original scientific objectives retained; affected BUILD obligations OPEN.** This is a reconciliation of the existing findings, owning roadmaps and accepted assignments, with the agreed portfolio priority order below. The publication remediation plan §0–1 and PD-5 govern. Accurate source/algebra corrections remain; they do not discharge missing scientific construction. No scientific deferral or reduced scope is approved here.

## Remediation priority and resume point

Agreed 3 November 2026. **Lead bundle: L1. Next delivery milestone: an independently verified L1 candidate through the canonical gates and ready for final human review.** This queue sets remediation order. Submission order remains an operator decision under the [publication strategy](../../PAPER_STRATEGY.md#3-sequencing--order-of-submission-and-dependencies).

1. **L1** — Swarm B3 susceptibility normalization and window, microscopic dynamics and matter coupling, and physical transport/frequency matching with units and error bounds. Start from the recorded regulated-scalar construction. Run claim, figure and manuscript work alongside the builds, then reevaluate the affected stages on the integrated candidate.
2. **L3** — Reconcile Paper 27's historical acceptance with L3's current findings and exact proof backing. Polish L3 and record the accepted evidence D3 can consume. Start the reconciliation in parallel as capacity permits.
3. **D3** — Integrate the accepted L3 content and close D3's own scientific and propagation findings.
4. **L2 → D2** — Advance the letter and its deep companion in that order. Dispatch shared proof and review work once.
5. **D1 → E1/E2** — Lead with the shared physics. Track one dispersive derivation across all three consumers, with separate device calibration, transmission and detection-band obligations.

**Parallel integration checkpoint: NJL/ADW.** Resume the reviewed NJL root/bracket repair from its existing qualification record. Use the exact test differential to isolate remaining merge blockers and deliver the qualified checkpoint incrementally to main. Keep the separate ADW scalar-domain and microscopic correspondence findings open through their own acceptance process.

The critical NJL finding has a canonical closure on the candidate at
[`b92e8db5c`](https://github.com/NetRxn/SK_EFT_Hawking/commit/b92e8db5c).
Remote readback on November 4 confirmed [draft PR #94](https://github.com/NetRxn/SK_EFT_Hawking/pull/94)
at `115e00122` contains that closure. Its main base `ce194cdc4` lacks
the closure commit. CodeQL passed at the draft head. An exact-head
focused run passed all 200 tests in `test_tetrad_gap.py`,
`test_tetrad_gap_solver.py`, and `test_adw.py`. The formula and solver
imports resolved to the candidate checkout. The broad test receipt
in the PR predates subsequent edits. The next delivery
checkpoint requires a current repository merge-gate result and a
separate disposition of any failures against the exact main base.

The [narrow NJL exact-pair readback](NJL_NARROW_EXACT_PAIR_R1.zip) compares the 20-path candidate `b92e8db5c` with actual main `ce194cdc4`. All 200 candidate physics tests passed with zero regressions among shared IDs. The old universal cutoff assertion was replaced by independently referenced above-cutoff cases. A count control passed on both after isolating the UV cache. The critical finding reads fixed. The narrow history is reachable from the current 266-path draft PR94. Its exact scoped qualification is separate from the older failing full merge profile and does not qualify either head for promotion.

Use the existing continuity notebooks and assignment records for exact candidates, finding IDs, owners, independent reviewers and next actions. Dispatch fresh cloud agents through Dot using the repository's agent roles where inputs can travel. Use controlled local capacity for Lean, local computations and untracked research as required.

**Lean substrate discovery before proof dispatch.** Follow the [Lean worker instructions](../../../.claude/plugins/skeft-qa/agents/lean-worker.md) and invoke the [Lean 4 skill](https://github.com/cameronfreer/lean4-skills/blob/main/plugins/lean4/skills/lean4/SKILL.md). Use the installed skill locally or the upstream instructions in a fresh cloud session. Pin the candidate commit, check its imports, and reconcile built dependencies against `lean/lean_deps.json`. Search project and Mathlib declarations by statement and type using Lean LSP and the skill's search tools. Check likely matches in the actual consumer import context. Older text searches can miss fully qualified names and signatures that begin on a subsequent line. Verify any “follow-on pending” docstring against current declarations and Git history. Record the checked commit, import context, matches, and remaining proof gap in the existing assignment or continuity notebook before opening a new proof task. If LSP or the pinned dependencies are unavailable, record the search as incomplete.

Record agreed priority changes here. If L1 needs a longer research cycle, keep its construction active and record the decision to move L3 into the lead delivery position. Order the remaining bundles before this queue runs low, using the publication strategy and canonical readiness evidence. This entry records priorities and milestones. New dispatches and acceptance receipts belong in their existing records.

and changed-key controls to the original loader. The loader determines

different keys requires a separate witness.

rows with 1,732,742 matched products already accepted. The refused pair

Their quotient stages took 33.08 and 57.54 seconds. This paired pilot
establishes exact input reuse and shows why input assembly alone is a
limited optimization target. There is no matched cached-versus-uncached
speedup measurement or proof of reuse of a later signed state.

The exact Group 672 scalar and its original evidence remain at the

### L1 bounded signed quotient reuse test

The [guarded pilot](L1_N16_C0_SIGNED_QUOTIENT_CACHE_PILOT_R1.zip)
runs the original two ordered quotient sweeps with a cache keyed by the
two ordered immutable signed factor identities and their exact closed
shared axes. The original function computes every cache miss. The cache
holds at most two million row references in its memoized output pairs.
The independent review reproduced exact hits and exercised differing
axis closure, sign, direction, retained boundary and eviction. Its
adverse test also demonstrates why the pilot requires exclusive ownership
of each backing dictionary. A read-only view cannot prevent a caller
with a retained mutable alias from changing a cached input. The actual
pilot creates those dictionaries privately, and the original quotient
uses replacement tables for its output. The pilot also keeps its raw
input tuples alive for the entire run. The independent adverse control
reproduced a stale token when a raw input was released and its identity
was reused. Both ownership and object lifetime are required for this key. The row-reference bound is
a cache policy and does not bound total RSS.

All 88 signed tables matched the original uncached frontier in each of
Groups 672 and 934. The trial observed zero hits across 2,048 quotient
calls. The two recorded quotient durations were 33.60 and 58.64 seconds.
The prior raw-input-only pilot measured 33.08 and 57.54 seconds on the
same respective groups. These are observed durations, with no controlled
speedup claimed. Peak process RSS in the bounded intermediate-cache trial
was 2.43 GB. A shared-input census identifies 55 of 256 linked pairs with
both raw tables shared between these groups. None is among the eleven
pairs whose two operands are untouched on first encounter in the original
sweep order. The raw-key overlap does not make an intermediate cache
effective in this traversal at this payload bound.

A separate ordered-topology replay groups the eleven untouched-input pairs
by their original family/block keys at their first encounter. At this
point the two source tables and degree context are fixed by the original
inputs. The original two directed quotients were evaluated once for each
of 252 exact classes covering 42,592 pair visits. The inputs carry
1,016,982,208 rows counted by reference and 5,402,696 rows counted once
per class, before counting the second directed operation. The recorded
batch produced 3,978,328 output row references, with at most 101,184 in
one pair. It took 14.66 seconds and peaked at 159.5 MB RSS while streaming
the classes. These are scoped potential savings for those specific inputs.
They do not establish the cost of holding batched outputs or of the full
quotient and terminal contraction. The next integration check is exact
substitution at the corresponding original sweep position. Independent review derived the incidence
order and class census, and checked a signed two-direction control against
the literal boundary contraction. That review did not rerun all 252
numerical classes or the recorded timing.

On the separate maximum-row Group 934 route, the smallest supported
joint-L assignment is 4, with 291,228 initial signed rows. Its original
conditioned guard reached 20 factors and 1,346,656 saved rows in
33.62 seconds. Three further exact passes took 66.67 summed process
seconds. The resulting real raw numerator is
`17102917328091839619858991015219058648224125577663565495349018624`
at inherited factor denominator `10925963483957527547486875425536212992`,
with zero imaginary part. Independent replay checked all source support
and signed rows, the 68 retained guarded joins, all 20 saved cap tables,
and all three continuation checkpoints. The other seven assignments and
the exact normalized full group remain open.
